--- A Handful of Dust.txt ---

[3] The Unspeakable L75
Overview Deserts preserve secrets, and the dusty wastelands of New Mexico have buried many. Nearby the test site of the world's first atomic bomb, a centuries-old coven of witches has been resurrected, and Delta Green has been alerted to the emerging threat. Dispatched to the scene, the investigating agents are yet to learn that a far older radioactive horror may overshadow all their expectations. Publishers description The story was originally written as a tournament game for the Melbourne, Australia tournament Conquest 1994 , hosted by The Cthulhu Conglomerate. As this predated any mainstream release of Delta Green material (that setting being confined to fanzine releases in The Unspeakable Oath at the time) the original story was devoid of any references to the setting. When it was published in 2003 by Yog-Sothoth. com, the original plot - which involved the players taking part in an FBI investigation - was transplanted into a Delta Green background.

--- At the Mountains of Murkiness.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L53
Clarke . A parody of H. P. Lovecraft At the Mountains of Madness , it was first published in the March 1940 edition of fanzine The Satellite Synopsis The sole remaining survivor of an expedition to the Antarctic tells of how his group stumbled across the ancient civilisation of the Elder Ones, existing quietly for millennia beneath Mount Morgue. Although at first believing their hosts to be cultured and genial, the party soon come to the realisation that the Elder Ones wish to destroy the human race. Making a bid for freedom, they are pursued by the unspeakable creatures which serve the Elder Ones, but manage to effect their escape with the aid of a shoddily-repaired main gateway which buys them time to flee. Behind the Mythos "At the Mountains of Murkiness" contains many elements from Lovecraft's works, all of them altered or exaggerated for comedic effect. These include a creature which fits the description of a Shoggoth , ancient bas-reliefs which are hard for the human mind to look at, an ill-fated Antarctic expedition , and the Elder Ones themselves- who are mentioned to own a half interest in pulp magazine Weird Tales .

--- Blair Reynolds.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L63
Reynolds Connection to the Mythos Artist Author Gaming supplement contributor Nationality United States Blair E. Reynolds (died 12 August 2021) was an American artist who also contributed material to at least one roleplaying game supplement, and wrote at least one short Cthulhu Mythos story. His output mostly appeared in supplements for Chaosium Call of Cthulhu horror roleplaying game produced by Pagan Publishing , and related material including periodicals, fiction anthologies, and comic books. Reynolds died on 12 August 2021 of congenital heart failure. Selected Works Short Fiction "Operation LOOKING GLASS" (1998) Gaming Material The Mysteries of Mesoamerica (2008, contributor) Artwork Periodicals The Unspeakable Oath (1991 - ? , covers) Anthologies Courting Madness (1992, cover) Gaming Supplements Tales of the Miskatonic Valley (1991, interior) Alone on Halloween (1992) The Resurrected Volume One: Grace Under Pressure (1993, interior) Walker in the Wastes (1994, cover) The Realm of Shadows (1997, cover and interior) The Resurrected III: Out of the Vault (2002, interior) The Mysteries of Mesoamerica (2008, cover and interior)

Последний в древнем роду наследник пришел в фамильную гробницу, чтобы узнать последний секрет своего рода... 1.0 — верстка, вычитка, аннотация, дизайн обложки — Isais. Роберт БлохСекрет в гробнице Роберт Блох Секрет в гробнице Ветер жутко завывал над полуночным склепом. Луна висела, как золотая летучая мышь, над древними могилами, проглядывая сквозь бледный туман своим блестящим подслеповатым глазом. Бесплотные ужасы могли таиться среди окруженных кедрами гробниц или ползать невидимые глазу среди затененных кенотафий, потому что это была не освященная земля. Но гробницы хранят свои жуткие секреты, и есть тайны, более черные, чем ночь, и более прокаженные, чем бледная луна. Именно в поисках таких тайн я и пришел, один и таясь от всех, в мой родовой склеп в полночь.

[2] Туман L13
Именно в поисках таких тайн я и пришел, один и таясь от всех, в мой родовой склеп в полночь. Мои предки были чародеями и колдунами в прежние времена, поэтому лежали отдельно от мест упокоения других людей, вот здесь, в этом крошащемся мавзолее в забытом людьми месте, окруженном только могилами своих слуг. Но не все слуги лежали здесь, потому что были и те, кто не умер. Сквозь туман я шел туда, где рухнувший могильник вырисовывался среди нависающих деревьев. Ветер усилился, когда я прошел по еле видной тропинке к сводчатому входу, погасив с яростью мой фонарь. Только луна продолжала освещать мой путь своим жутким светом. Таким образом я добрался до азотистого, поросшего плесневыми грибами портала семейного склепа. Здесь свет луны заблестел на двери, которая не была похожа на другие двери, — огромной массивной плите из железа, вмурованной в крепкие гранитные стены.

[5] Туман L33
Поэтому я отправился, один и без оружия, в одинокий лес и к тому месту, где должен встретить свою судьбу. Луна поднялась над поместьем, когда я уходил, но я не оглядывался назад. Я видел ее отражение в водах ручья, который проложил путь между деревьями, и в ее свете вода была похожа на кровь. Затем туман поднялся тихо от болота, и желтый призрачный свет разлился по небу, заманивая меня за стену черных и жирных деревьев, ветви которых, раскачиваемые унылым ветром, тихо касались отдаленной гробницы. Корни и лианы лезли мне под ноги, виноградные лозы и кустарники хватали мое тело, но в моих ушах громом отдавался призыв, который нельзя описать и который нельзя "отменить" природой или человеком. В тот миг, когда я нерешительно замер в дверном проеме, миллионы идиотских голосов забормотали приглашение войти, чему смертный разум не мог противостоять.

[9] Туманом L45
Две когтистые лапы, холодные, как пламя ледяного ада, сомкнулись вокруг моего горла, два глаза проникли, словно личинки, через мою обезумевшую сущность, смех, порожденный безумием, одиноко звучал в моих ушах, словно гром судьбы. Костяные пальцы вонзились в мои глаза и ноздри, делая меня совершенно беспомощным, в то время как желтые клыки щелкали все ближе и ближе у моего горла. Мир свернулся, обернутый туманом огненной смерти. Внезапно чары разрушились. Я оторвал взгляд от этого слюнявого, злого лица, и мгновенно, как катастрофическая вспышка света, пришло понимание. Сила этого существа была чисто ментальной — в одиночестве мои несчастные родственники приходили сюда, и только в одиночестве могли противостоять и пытаться освободиться от силы ужасных глаз монстра, великий боже! Был ли я жертвой гниющей мумии? Моя правая рука опустилась, нанеся удар ужасу между глаз.

Роберт БлохШорохи в подвале Роберт Блох Шорохи в подвале (Выползающий из склепа) В Аркхеме, где древние фронтоны указывают, словно пальцы колдунов, в небеса, рассказывают странные истории. Но, по сути, странные истории всегда актуальны в Аркхеме. Есть истории о каждом гниющем здании, истории о каждом маленьком, похожем на глаз мертвеца окошке, смотрящем в сторону моря, когда спускается туман. Здесь невероятная фантазия, кажется, процветает, вскормленная сморщенными колдунами самого города, напитанная сухими кладбищенскими легендами и стекающимися в эти темные норы суевериями. Когда-то Аркхем был довольно странным местом, обитель ведьм и колдунов, дерзких и дьявольских. В древние времена люди короля очистили город от колдовства. И снова в 1818 году новое правительство вмешалось, чтобы уничтожить некоторые особенно жестокие убежища в наиболее древних домах и, кстати, раскопать кладбище, которое лучше было оставить нетронутым.

--- Byakhee.txt ---

[2] The Unspeakable L109
H. P. Lovecraft The Festival Byakhee are fictional creatures in the Cthulhu Mythos . They are predators who are known to travel in the vacuum of interstellar space. If one has the means by which to survive the myriad threats space presents, then one could ride a Byakhee to anywhere in the cosmos. When travelling beyond the speed of light, the creature's metabolism is greatly increased. When descending to the surface of any given planet, it is usually to feed, at which point it is best avoided. Overview This interstellar race is believed to serve Hastur the Unspeakable and may be summoned to partake in rituals. Composed of conventional matter, they are vulnerable to ordinary mundane weapons but possess cunning intelligence and guile, and some are able to cast spells. Dwelling in interstellar space, Byakhee do not have bases on Earth (although the Nameless City serves as a sort of harbour for them), but may be summoned to perform deeds or to serve as steeds, carrying riders through interstellar space - such riders may need to partake of the revered Space Mead to survive such cold journeys.

[2] Туманами L39
В любом случае, чувствовал он, он в выигрыше. Он получит желаемое или же предоставит подходящую жертву для младшего бога. Что касается двух воров, у них были свои собственные идеи. За столом они обедали редкими зверями. Зата любил откармливать свою добычу. Он внимательно слушал план воров по осуществлению его предложения, не будучи полностью уверенным в его успехе, но снова думая о преимуществе, которое можно извлечь даже из возможной неудачи. Он всё же не смог удержаться от комментария. "Клянусь туманами тёмных гор, я понятия не имею, что сказать на ваши предложения." Зата рассмеялся, и складки его необъятного живота заколыхались взад-вперёд, словно морские волны. "Я так и думал, — ответил Ло Пферзонн. — Я не ожидал твёрдого ответа сразу же." "Это так, но—" Прерванный ходом своих мыслей, взгляд Заты переместился за окно столовой, где он увидел пару торговцев с серебряного рынка через дорогу, дерущихся перед таверной. Двое серебряных дел мастеров уже изрядно приложились к бутылке и начали отвешивать друг другу пощёчины. Казалось, они вот-вот устроят серьёзный переполох, вызвав большое оживление среди прочих посетителей таверны.


--- Cave beast.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L65
The Rats in the Walls " , H. P. Lovecraft The thing will haunt me, for who can say the extermination is complete, and that analogous phenomena do not exist all over the world? Who can, with my knowledge, think of the earth's unknown caverns without a nightmare dread of future possibilities? I cannot see a well or a subway entrance without shuddering . . . why cannot the doctors give me something to make me sleep, or truly calm my brain when it thunders? What I saw in the glow of flashlight after I shot the unspeakable straggling object was so simple that almost a minute elapsed before I understood and went delirious. The object was nauseous; a filthy whitish gorilla thing with sharp yellow fangs and matted fur. It was the ultimate product of mammalian degeneration; the frightful outcome of isolated spawning, multiplication, and cannibal nutrition above and below the ground; the embodiment of all the snarling and chaos and grinning fear that lurk behind life. It had looked at me as it died, and its eyes had the same odd quality that marked those other eyes which had stared at me underground and excited cloudy recollections.

--- Courting Madness.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L69
НАЗВАНИЕ: Courting Madness ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:14:05 СИМВОЛОВ: 1645 Courting Madness Author Various Illustrator Blair Reynolds Jeff Barber Charlie Mitchell Cover Artist Blair Reynolds Country United States Language English Series Call of Cthulhu Genre(s) Roleplaying game Publisher Pagan Publishing Release Date Pages Courting Madness is an anthology collection of material for Chaosium Call of Cthulhu horror roleplaying game. Compiled by Pagan Publishing for GenCon 1992 - from which it was ultimately banned as a result of the suggestive nature of Blair Reynolds' cover art - the book contained resources collected from issues 1 - 3 of gaming magazine The Unspeakable Oath , all of which was first published in 1991. The production team for Courting Madness included Brian Bevel, John Tynes , and Brian Appleton ; cover art was provided by Blair Reynolds , with interior panels by Reynolds, Jeff Barber , and Charlie Mitchell. It ran to a total of 48 pages, and was released in an extremely limited run of 200 copies. Introduction: "The Dread Page Of Azathoth" (by John Tynes "Yeah, But What Does It Look Like?"

--- Creatures & Cultists.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L69
Todd Kingrea . It was issued as a book containing rules and player sheets, with the cards themselves needing to be cut out after purchase. Gameplay involves each player assembling a cult which they then use to battle other cults. Once a set number of points have been achieved through winning battles, the player may then attempt to summon their dark god and win the game. First appearing in 1990 in issue #4 of Mythos gaming magazine The Unspeakable Oath , at that time edited by John Tynes, Creatures & Cultists was later released through Tynes' label Pagan Publishing as a stand-alone game in 1993. It is currently out of print.

--- Cthulhu Mythos in Film & Television.txt ---

[6] The Unnamed One L865
Because of the child’s dissemblance to all other spawn of Cthulhu, its identity was assumed to be Cthylla . However, in the official comic "Rick and Morty vs. Cthulhu", it is the son of Cthylla and the grandson of Cthulhu, named Thoolie Smith. Robot Chicken (2005-present): the 2016 episode "The Unnamed One" featured Cthulhu as a presidential candidate, a nod to the Cthulhu for President parody campaigns begun by Chaosium in 1996. In the segment, Cthulhu promotes his candidacy on The Oprah Show but ends up killing Oprah, after she questioned if he was eligible to run due to him being a resident of R'lyeh .

[8] The Nameless One L1145
The Void (2016; Steven Kostanski and Jeremy Gillespie): a supernatural horror film featuring people under siege by cultists in a hospital on the site of a dimensional portal; characters transform into eldritch horrors over the course of the film. TV American Dad! (2005-present): In the episode "Echoes", B'thazalom the Nameless One is thematically similar to the Great Old Ones , being a malevolent extra-dimensional entity who was sealed away in the Channel 3 News Station Doppler radar, and will bring destruction to the world once awoken. Its moniker is also a reference to several of Lovecraft's works, such as " The Nameless City Beast Wars: Transformers (1996-1999): The Vok are Lovecraftian in theme, right down to their true forms and motivations being incomprehensible to lesser beings.

--- Cult of the Bloody Tongue.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L93
The rites take place in the cellar, which has been suitably enlarged for such use. The shop is run by Silas N'Kwane, while the sect's sorcerer is Mukumga N'Dari. Assassins of the Bloodtongue often use the pranga (an African long machete) in their attacks, although American members have sometimes used pistols. The assassins or executioners of the sect wear a terrifying-looking cap, with a red band that hangs from the forehead. Notable members Mukunga M’Dari : High Priest of the Cult of the Bloody Tongue in his New York branch. This powerful enemy enjoys the unspeakable eliminating the adversaries of his god. He knows the location of Mountain of the Black Wind, he knows what is hidden in the temple cavern, and he has some clues about plan of Nyarlathotep, but he will never reveal this information to anyone unless he is magically compelled. If he is about to die, his last act will be to contact Nyarlathotep to ask him to avenge him.

He was the originator of The Shadow Over Usenet, a detailed online discussion of the works of H. P. Lovecraft. --- Dark One (Cthulhu Dark Ages).txt ---

[1] Magnum Innominandum L41
НАЗВАНИЕ: Dark One (Cthulhu Dark Ages) ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:15:39 СИМВОЛОВ: 1946 For other uses of the term see Dark One (disambiguation) Dark One Sociocultural Characteristics Also known as Dark Folk Miri Nigri Children of the Fire Mist Dwarfs Black-elves Afanc Korr Habitat Parasitic Affiliations Nyarlathotep Magnum Innominandum Real World Information First appearance EXP Cthulhu Dark Ages Created by Stéphane Gesbert In the role-playing game Cthulhu Dark Ages , the Dark Ones , or Dark Folk , are a species of subterranean creatures who serve Nyarlathotep (a. k. a. Loki) and the Magnum Innominandum . They are usually encountered in groups of ten or more. Overview The Dark Ones correspond to the dwarfs of Norse and Germanic mythology, and due to their alliance with Nyarlathotep, have been speculated to correspond to the Million Favoured Ones as well. Their real form is unknown, as they are in fact parasitic creatures that inhabit the dead bodies of other beings, which they are capable of reanimating.

[2] Magnum Innominandum L73
НАЗВАНИЕ: Dark One (Cthulhu Dark Ages) ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:15:39 СИМВОЛОВ: 1946 For other uses of the term see Dark One (disambiguation) Dark One Sociocultural Characteristics Also known as Dark Folk Miri Nigri Children of the Fire Mist Dwarfs Black-elves Afanc Korr Habitat Parasitic Affiliations Nyarlathotep Magnum Innominandum Real World Information First appearance EXP Cthulhu Dark Ages Created by Stéphane Gesbert In the role-playing game Cthulhu Dark Ages , the Dark Ones , or Dark Folk , are a species of subterranean creatures who serve Nyarlathotep (a. k. a. Loki) and the Magnum Innominandum . They are usually encountered in groups of ten or more. Overview The Dark Ones correspond to the dwarfs of Norse and Germanic mythology, and due to their alliance with Nyarlathotep, have been speculated to correspond to the Million Favoured Ones as well. Their real form is unknown, as they are in fact parasitic creatures that inhabit the dead bodies of other beings, which they are capable of reanimating. When their current host becomes too decayed, the Dark Ones must find themselves a new one to inhabit.

--- Daughter of the Elk Goddess_2 [Перевод [Перевод.txt].txt].txt ---

[2] Туман L83
Все больше косматых тварей спускалось на перевал, и рабы собрались вокруг принцессы, размахивая копьями, данными им солдатами. Они станут последней защитой Квархи, если воины не смогут отразить зверолюдей. Люди Иккуа умирали быстро, когти вурми были слишком быстры для их клинков, слишком сильны против их щитов. Капитан Оолзар сумел сразить груду волосатых каннибалов, но никто из его солдат не был равен ему в бою. Храбрые люди погибали в алой агонии, пока их предводитель продолжал сражаться. Когда Атанеку размозжил череп разъяренного вурми в красный туман, он наконец услышал крик принцессы. Это отвлечение повернуло его голову достаточно надолго, чтобы раздутый вурми врезался в него. Его когти глубоко вонзились в руки охотника, когда тот кубарем покатился с ним по снегу. Тварь дышала адской вонью ему в лицо, пока земля уходила у них из-под ног. Сцепившись в смертельном захвате с рычащей тварью, Атанеку рухнул за край глубокой расселины. Как глубоко должно быть падение, он не мог знать, но он пытался выжать жизнь из зловонного вурми, даже пока они крутились и падали. Белая земля устремилась вверх, чтобы встретить его.

[5] Тумана L125
На грубой каменной глыбе перед идолом и его слюнявой алтарной чашей лежало бесчувственное тело Квархи, лишенное ее королевских одеяний и бледное от морозного воздуха пещер. Знаки дурного обращения лежали на ее теле в виде синяков и царапин, но она казалась живой. Атанеку предположил, что ее спокойное состояние было результатом дурманящего сна. Без сомнения, вурми разбудили бы ее в кульминации церемонии, чтобы их бог мог насладиться своей жертвой живой и извивающейся. Охотник обрушился на танцующих вурми, словно вихрь, несущий смерть из слоновой кости. Облака кровавого тумана вздымались за ним, и жрец карабкался следом, перерезая глотки впавшим в транс вурми кинжалом и копьем. Косматые твари были настолько поглощены своей церемонией, что не сопротивлялись продвижению Атанеку к алтарю. Дюжина превращенных в месиво вурми отметила путь охотника, когда он встал перед гранитной плитой, где беспомощно лежала принцесса. Атанеку подхватил ее хрупкое тело и положил его на свое левое плечо, орудуя своей палицей-бивнем теперь одной рукой. Но ни один вурми не бросился вперед, чтобы бросить ему вызов. Зверолюди корчились на полу, словно клубок змей, с пеной у рта и изрыгая гортанные проклятия. Их пение превратилось в хор стонущих, хрюкающих непристойностей.

А уж в каждую ночь зимнего солнцестояния и в каждую вальпургиеву ночь обязательно пропадало несколько юных девиц. Люди Скаи увязали первое — с появлением полузвериных-получеловеческих следов в своих садах, а второе — со странными огнями и барабанным боем на удаленных холмах. Последний раз воины Скаи бросили вызов 'Иджироту во время правления короля Пнила Ультарского. На битву отправились все способные носить оружие мужчины, и на этот раз, кроме обычного военного снаряжения, они использовали заклятия и заговоры древних.

[6] Туман L71
Он читал про Других Богов, которые некогда правили и будут править снова, об Азатоте, столепестковом возбудителе импульсов хаоса, о Йог-Созоте, всепроникающем ужасе, гнездящемся во внутренних сферах бытия, о Ниарлототепе, который иногда принимает некую, задрапированную желтым шелком, форму, а иногда воплощается в иллюзии, помрачающие разум. Он читал, как эти боги и демоны награждают избранных, ставших слепыми орудиями их воли, и как они карают неверных слуг. Под конец он прочел омерзительный пассаж, вырезанный безукоризненными письменами на радужной каменной доске, более твердой, чем гранатовый браслет Нилрона. Речь шла о шутке Ниарлототепа, приславшего своим креатурам вместо себя полуродственника и другое лицо, безумное и прожорливое создание, способное излучать непереносимый, как ядовитый туман, ужас. Едва Нилрон закончил читать, его факел яростно замерцал — прогорел почти дотла. Нилрон представил себе путь назад, сквозь лабиринт, и его проняла дрожь. Самочувствие Нилрона не улучшилось, когда, проходя мимо алтаря, он увидел на плоском камне, закрывающем неизвестно куда ведущий ход, особый символ и заметил, что мох вокруг камня совсем недавно был вытоптан. Путь назад оказался ошеломляюще трудным. Он вышел к основанию ведущей вверх спиральной лестницы как раз тогда, когда факел мигнул в последний раз и потух.


--- De_Vermis_Mysteriis_The_H.P._Lovecraft_Wiki_Fandom_a6d3e20889.txt ---

(EXP: The Keeper's Companion) References to Mythos entities[] Outer Gods[] Iod (EXP: The Grand Grimoire of Cthulhu Mythos Magic) Magnum Innominandum (CIRCLE: "The Shambler from the Stars") Nyarlathotep (CIRCLE: "The Faceless God") Great Old Ones[] Byatis (CIRCLE: "The Shambler from the Stars") Han (CIRCLE: "The Shambler from the Stars") Sebek (CIRCLE: "The Secret of Sebek") Tsathoggua (CIRCLE: "The Shambler from the Stars") Yig (CIRCLE: "The Shambler from the Stars") History[] De Vermis Mysteriis was written in Latin by Ludvig Prinn in prison circa 1542. A more historically likely date of 1484 has also been given. (EXP: The Encyclopedia Cthulhiana). The manuscript was smuggled out and published in Cologne in 1543, a year after Prinn's execution. In 1569, the book was banned by Pope Pius V and almost all copies of it were rapidly destroyed. (CIRCLE: "The Shambler from the Stars") Existing copies[] At least 15 copies are known to have survived (EXP: The Keeper's Companion).

[6] Magnum Innominandum L451
Bloch, a teenager in 1935, corresponded with Lovecraft about his story "The Shambler from the Stars" prior to its publication, in part to get permission to kill off a character based on the older writer. While giving his enthusiastic blessing, Lovecraft also suggested that the book featured in the story, named by Bloch as Mysteries of the Worm, be referred to instead by the Latin equivalent De Vermis Mysteriis. Lovecraft also provided Bloch with a bit of Latin to use as an invocation from the book: "Tibi, Magnum Innominandum, signa stellarum nigrarum et bufaniformis Sadoquae sigilum"--which can be translated as "To you, the great Not-to-Be-Named, signs of the black stars, and the seal of the toad-shaped Tsathoggua".


--- Delta Green (setting).txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L69
Premise Delta Green is a top-secret, black-ops organisation dedicated to combatting the threats presented by various Cthulhu Mythos creatures and cults from the shadows. Originally taking place in a 1990s setting, it has since been updated to reflect contemporary times, equipment and issues, and expanded to cover other time periods, such as the Vietnam War. Development Created by John Scott Tynes Dennis Detwiller and Adam Scott Glancy , the very first appearance of Delta Green was in a roleplaying game scenario in the Cthulhu Mythos gaming fanzine The Unspeakable Oath , by Pagan Publishing . The scenario was titled "Convergence" and was released in 1992. Pagan followed this up in 1997 with a complete sourcebook for the setting, which itself spawned a series of expansions. In 2016, Arc Dream Publishing released a stand-alone roleplaying game based on Delta Green, which was also accompanied by a wealth of satellite books.


--- Delta Green (supplement).txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L73
It was published by third-party content provider Pagan Publishing in 1997. Overview Delta Green is a contemporary setting for Call of Cthulhu , and involves players taking on the roles of agents of the shadowy government organisation from which it takes its name. The organisation is dedicated to combatting any paranormal threats which present themselves to humanity, and ensuring that the nature of these threats remains unknown to the general public. The setting was developed from a single scenario printed in The Unspeakable Oath , a Pagan Publishing fanzine dedicated to Call of Cthulhu , titled "Convergence". Delta Green has since become one of the most popular Call of Cthulhu settings, and now boasts its own stand-alone game system (albeit one which is still compatible with other editions), a host of supplements spanning an in-universe time period of several decades, and dedicated fiction. These have all been published through the Pagan label.


--- Dennis Detwiller.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L41
НАЗВАНИЕ: Dennis Detwiller ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:15:59 СИМВОЛОВ: 2075 Dennis Detwiller Connection to the Mythos Games Designer Birth Date 12 July 1972 Nationality United States Years Active 1991-present Website Dennis Detwiller (born 12 July 1972) is an American traditional and video games creator, author, editor, and occasional artist. Overview Beginning his gaming career by volunteering at Pagan Publishing on The Unspeakable Oath magazine, Detwiller subsequently became the company's art director, and co-created the award-winning Delta Green roleplaying setting with Adam Scott Glancy and Pagan founder John Scott Tynes After leaving Pagan Publishing, Detwiller co-founded Arc Dream Publishing with Shane Ivey ; Arc Dream acquired the license for The Unspeakable Oath and revived it, and still produces books for the Delta Green roleplaying game (the original book having been a setting supplement for Call of Cthulhu Detwiller has since mostly left the traditional games industry and now works as video games designer, although he does contribute the occasional book to the Delta Green roleplaying universe. Говоря прямо, казалось абсурдом, что я вообще стал над этим задумываться. Ведь это было не больше чем сном, привидевшимся мне в течение ночи. [11] Тумана L137

Это и было вторым случаем воспроизведения чего-то постороннего моему личному опыту. Я, разумеется, слышал о психическом осадке — остаточных силах, оседающих на месте любого события, будь то ужасная трагедия или просто какое-либо мощное эмоциональное переживание, свойственное человеку вообще, — любовь, ненависть, страх.

Joseph Payne Brennan EXP The Feaster from Afar The Feaster from Afar is a fictional entity created by Joseph Payne Brennan for his eponymous short story Description The Feaster from Afar is an avatar of Hastur the Unspeakable and a fearsome predator that moves through interstellar space. At some point, a branch of the Whateley family seems to have summoned it down to Earth , where it has remained, inhabiting the hills outside Granbury, New England, turning them into a barren waste completely devoid of animal life. The Feaster from Afar glides over the terrain, unaffected by gravity, and might be able to pass through solid objects as well.

[ Beyond2 . . . quotations from such books as the Comte d’Erlette’s Cultes des Goules the Pnakotic Manuscript the Libor Ivonie and the Unaussprechlichen Kulten of Von Junzt . . . [ Curwen It was the volume of unnameable confessions by the monk, Clithanus . . . . It was the story of the return of the Evil Ones to earth, and the ruin they had worked. Luridly, the monk had painted the pictures of the unspeakable crime and lust with which the earth had been cursed. Then, more briefly, more vaguely, he told how holy men from the interior of Europe had driven these evil genii into the waters of the earth. The holy men had used the forces of the Elder Gods, the power of the Ancient Ones, which they had come upon in some manner.

The American writers are probably the Lovecraft circle, along with a few precursors such as Robert W. Chambers and Ambrose Bierce I refer the skeptical to the occurrence at Devil Reef off Innsmouth and direct their attention to those curiously batrachian survivals one may yet find in isolated places in the vicinity of both Innsmouth and Newburyport, as well as to the thinly disguised fiction of it by the late H. P. Lovecraft . [ Curwen In Derleth's world, a Lovecraft-like character named Ward Phillips writes fiction about the Cthulhu Mythos: He [Ward Phillips] grew older, and his fictions found their way into print, and the myths of Cthulhu ; of Hastur the Unspeakable! of Yog-Sothoth ; and Shub-Niggurath , the Black Goat of the Woods with a Thousand Young; of Hypnos , the god of sleep; of the Great Old Ones and their messenger, who was Nyarlathotep —all became part of the lore of Phillips’ innermost being, and of the shadow-world beyond. [2] The Unspeakable L137
[Beyond2] . . . quotations from such books as the Comte d’Erlette’s Cultes des Goules, the Pnakotic Manuscript, the Libor Ivonie, and the Unaussprechlichen Kulten of Von Junzt . . . [Curwen] It was the volume of unnameable confessions by the monk, Clithanus. . . . It was the story of the return of the Evil Ones to earth, and the ruin they had worked. Luridly, the monk had painted the pictures of the unspeakable crime and lust with which the earth had been cursed. Then, more briefly, more vaguely, he told how holy men from the interior of Europe had driven these evil genii into the waters of the earth. The holy men had used the forces of the Elder Gods, the power of the Ancient Ones, which they had come upon in some manner.

[3] The Unspeakable L255
The American writers are probably the Lovecraft circle, along with a few precursors such as Robert W. Chambers and Ambrose Bierce: I refer the skeptical to the occurrence at Devil Reef off Innsmouth and direct their attention to those curiously batrachian survivals one may yet find in isolated places in the vicinity of both Innsmouth and Newburyport, as well as to the thinly disguised fiction of it by the late H. P. Lovecraft. [Curwen] In Derleth's world, a Lovecraft-like character named Ward Phillips writes fiction about the Cthulhu Mythos: He [Ward Phillips] grew older, and his fictions found their way into print, and the myths of Cthulhu; of Hastur the Unspeakable! of Yog-Sothoth; and Shub-Niggurath, the Black Goat of the Woods with a Thousand Young; of Hypnos, the god of sleep; of the Great Old Ones and their messenger, who was Nyarlathotep—all became part of the lore of Phillips’ innermost being, and of the shadow-world beyond. [Lamp] Punishments for Those Who Reveal Too Much The cults of the Great Old Ones punish those who reveal their secrets, including notably Abdul Alhazred: "Can it be other than the Arab Alhazred?" . . . "Tortured and slain, beyond question," agreed Professor Shrewsbury calmly.


--- Fungi from Yuggoth.txt ---

[2] The Nameless One L79
II. Pursuit The narrator flees the shop, hiding the book under his coat. Despite not being seen stealing it, he can't shake the laugh from the shop, and the sound of approaching footsteps as the path ahead grows more and more unusual. III. The Key Making it home and bolting the door, the narrator reveals his intention to use the book to bridge dimensions in order to explain his unusual visions of sunset spires and twilight woods. IV. Recognition The narrator enters a vision of the "strange, grey world" of Yuggoth, where a victim on an altar to the Nameless One is being feasted upon by inhuman creatures. When the body shrieks at him with a "dead cry," the narrator recognizes it as himself! V. Homecoming A daemon tells the narrator that he was going to take him "home," and escorts him through a seaside city and into the sunset-lit sky, taking him to a black gulf he said "was his home when he had sight." VI. The Lamp The narrator enters "hollow cliffs" where "frightened hieroglyphs" warn all Earth creatures away.

--- GAME_027e43f62f.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L27
The investigators will meet a group of Hastur worshippers that have discovered a special plant from the rainforest that can induce people to dread dreams with He Who Is Not to be Named. Better than that, this plant is strongly addictive -- so, with a good distribution system, the cultists believe that they'll be able to enslave all mankind. As the search goes on, the investigators will have an opportunity to face the King in Yellow and, perhaps, the Unspeakable himself. The City and the Theater Just five years before the events in this scenario, Manaus was one of the most interesting cities of the world. The rubber tree, that existed only in the Brazilian rainforest, has made the fortune of a group of land owners, men and women who like to feel that they are in Paris, Rome or Lisbon, not in the heart of the wild forest. And for a time they had the money to buy a look of "European civilization," so they did it.

[4] The Unspeakable L257
There's a text about the pallid ceramic mask that can make a shaman receive and keep the soul of the King in Yellow -- hinting that the destruction of the mask will dismiss the avatar -- and about the sacred (or damned, depending on one's point of view) plant that, after boiled in water, allows normal men to make contact with the Great Old One. The book also predicts the "day when all the nations will be one with the Unspeakable." All the real and valuable information is hidden under vast layers of Christian mythology and metaphor, however, and it is very hard to find the Mythos truths behind it. Reading this book takes 15 weeks, costs 1d6/2d6 Sanity points and adds +10 points to the Cthulhu Mythos skill. The spell multiplier is only x1 (due to the oblique language) and the book contains Call and Dismiss Hastur, Summon/Bind Byakhee, Create Gate and up to seven other spells, chosen by the keeper.

Провожаемые улыбками актеров, мы вышли из здания театра на окутанные туманом улицы. — Дорогой друг, — начал я, — что бы вы там ни... — Ни слова! — сказал он. — В этом городе много ушей. И ни слова не было произнесено, покуда мы не наняли кеб. Забравшись внутрь, мы потряслись по Чаринг—Кросс—роуд.

[2] Тумана L61
Долго они тащились пешком по пустыне под палящим солнцем, жизнь им сохраняли лишь убывающая с каждым глотком вода и крохи провизии из сумки Яр Али. О том, чтобы найти легендарный город, они уже и не думали. Слепо тащились вперед и вперед в надежде наткнуться на источник. Поворачивать назад было бессмысленно. И они шли вперед. Стервятники в белых бурнусах налетели на них, вынырнув из окутавшего горизонт тумана. Укрывшись за невысоким, поспешно насыпанным валом, друзья начали перестрелку со всадниками, бешено носившимися вокруг них. Пули бедуинов пронизывали их ненадежный заслон, швыряли в глаза песок, отрывали клочья от одежды. Но счастье их не покинуло — друзья ни разу не были ранены. Что за идиотские были помыслы, подумал Стив.

Осторожно двинулись следом, и вы не услышали, словно слепые телята. И вдруг я узнаю тебя! — Вам просто повезло, — буркнул Стив. — Мы думали, что ни один бедуин не отважится войти в Кара-Шехр. Как раз легенда о красном камне и привела меня в эту запретную пустыню. Целый месяц я уговаривал моих людей идти сюда за мной. И вот я здесь. А ваше присутствие — милая для меня неожиданность. --- Hastur (Cthulhu Universalis) [Hastur_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_29511153b5.txt].txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L203
Hastur and the other Great Old Ones were defeated and cast out by the Elder Gods, but shall rise again [ Dweller Keeper Lurker Seal online text ), Sky ]. Unspeakable Hastur is often referred to as Hastur the Unspeakable, He Who Is Not To Be Named, or Him Who Is Not To Be Named [ Curwen Depths Dweller Gorge Hastur Keeper Lamp Lurker Middle Sky Space Valley ]. It is not clear in what sense he is unspeakable or unnameable, since numerous books and people refer to him by name. These epithets may be an application of the old saying "speak of the Devil and he will appear."

[4] The Unspeakable L501
Spawn and Servants Hastur is served by strange bat-like creatures (the Byakhee ) [ Dweller Gorge Sky ], who can travel in time and space [ Curwen ]. Hastur is mentioned in the invocation to call the Byakhee. [ Curwen Gorge Keeper Sky Island Father Brisbois referred to Hastur the Unspeakable and his loathly spawn [ Ithaqua ]. It is not clear if the "loathly spawn" are the Byakhee or other creatures. Cult There is a cult of Hastur that survives to the present day [ Curwen ]. Asaph Gilman was convinced that worship of Hastur was less wide-spread than that of Cthulhu.

[5] The Unspeakable L579
Henry Lucas had no training in Latin. We can only assume that Lucas was a Catholic or was influenced at some point by a Catholic person who had converted to worshipping Hastur. The Confessions of Clithanus includes a prayer to the Elder Gods to banish unspeakable Hastur and others. [ Depths online text )] References in Books The Necronomicon includes the lost and terrible legend of Hastur, the Unspeakable [ Depths online text ), Witches ]. The Necronomicon says that Hastur shall return from a dark star in the Hyades near Aldebaran Keeper ]. The R'lyeh Text also predicts Hastur's return from that dark star [ Sky ].

[8] The Unspeakable L629
The R'lyeh Text also predicts Hastur's return from that dark star [ Sky ]. Hastur is mentioned in one or more of these books: the Sussex Manuscript the Celaeno Fragments and the Cultes des Ghoules Gorge ]. Wilbur Akeley's books mentioned the unspeakable Hastur [ Gable online text )]. Other Mentions Hastur and Cthulhu struggled for possession of Paul Tuttle's body. Hastur succeeded in briefly incarnating in Paul Tuttle's body. One of the Elder Gods (1) then cast Tuttle/Hastur into the depths of space. As Tuttle/Hastur receded into the sky, listeners heard a wailing of Tekeli-li, tekeli-li, tekeli-li .

[9] The Unspeakable L697
The Nameless City is a domain of Hastur [ Keeper ]. Wilbur Akeley was concerned that Hastur might try to come through the transdimensional gable window [ Gable online text )]. Leander Alwyn left pages written in code that mentioned Hastur [ Beyond2 ]. Letters to Septimus Bishop hailed him in the name of Hastur the Unspeakable [ Middle ]. Sylvan Phillips wrote of Hastur [ Seal online text )]. Ward Phillips (1) wrote of Hastur the Unspeakable in his fictions [ Lamp online text )]. Amos Tuttle wrote of Hastur the Unspeakable [ Lurker ]. Other Sources Hastur was called on in prayers to bring the dead to life.

[10] The Unspeakable L715
Leander Alwyn left pages written in code that mentioned Hastur [ Beyond2 ]. Letters to Septimus Bishop hailed him in the name of Hastur the Unspeakable [ Middle ]. Sylvan Phillips wrote of Hastur [ Seal online text )]. Ward Phillips (1) wrote of Hastur the Unspeakable in his fictions [ Lamp online text )]. Amos Tuttle wrote of Hastur the Unspeakable [ Lurker ]. Other Sources Hastur was called on in prayers to bring the dead to life. The prayers also called on Nyarlathotep , suggesting that Hastur might be a synonym for Nyarlathotep or perhaps an ally [ HC Death online text ), Isle online text )].

[1] The Unspeakable L177
Nyaruko-san The Bride of the Yellow King The Baby In Yellow (Mentioned) Created by Ambrose Bierce Camilla : You, sir, should unmask. Stranger : Indeed? Cassilda : Indeed it’s time. We all have laid aside disguise but you. Stranger : I wear no mask. Camilla : (Terrified, aside to Cassilda.) No mask? No mask! ADJ The King in Yellow Hastur the Unspeakable is a fictional deity in the Cthulhu Mythos . Although the being is associated with H. P. Lovecraft , it is only mentioned in one of his novellas, The Whisperer in Darkness . It was originally the creation of Ambrose Bierce , whose short story " Haïta the Shepherd " describes Hastur as a god of shepherds.

--- High Priest Not to Be Described.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L193
He also jokingly stated in the same letter that Bloch was the "reincarnation of Meinheer Ludvig Prinn , author of De Vermis Mysteriis HPL Selected Letter 5.748) Very little is known about this creature: we know it is clad in a yellow robe and dwells deep in Earth's Dreamlands , served by the Moon-beast s, shantak birds, and the Men of Leng . Identity possibilities include: a moon-beast, an avatar of Nyarlathotep, or the King in Yellow, Hastur. The yellow could suggest connections to Hastur , the unspeakable, but since August Derleth is credited with the connection between the King in Yellow and Hastur after this story was written, it is likely a coincidence. More probable is that this is some aspect of Nyarlathotep meant to deal with dangerous dreamers but it could be noted, however, that Nyarlathotep does appear elsewhere in the story in its pharaoh form.

, first published in 2018. Overview Enter a midnight world of grotesque creatures and ancient deities from nightmare dimensions. HORRORS & ABOMINATIONS by Mark McLaughlin and Michael Sheehan, Jr. presents 24 stories of Lovecraftian fantasy and horror. Learn the unholy secrets of such malevolent beings as Cthulhu Yog-Sothoth Nyarlathotep , Azalareon, Mother Hydra , the King in Yellow , and Yagdolak. Publisher's release Consisting entirely of material reprinted from earlier collections, Horrors & Abominations runs to a total of 248 pages (311 as an ebook) and was released in paperback (December 2018), hardcover (December 2021) and digital (December 2021) editions. It is divided into four thematic sections.

--- In the Mad Mountains_ Stories Inspired by H. P. Lovecraft.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L49
Lansdale (Bubba Ho-tep) returns with this wicked short story collection of his irreverent Lovecraftian tributes. Lansdale is terrifyingly down-home while merging his classic gonzo stylings with the eldritch horrors of H. P. Lovecraft . Knowingly skewering Lovecraft’s paranoid mythos, Lansdale embarks upon haunting yet sly explorations of the unknown, capturing the essence of cosmic dread. A sinister blues recording pressed on vinyl in blood conjures lethal shadows with its unearthly wails. In order to rescue Tom Sawyer, Huck Finn traverses the shifting horrors of the aptly named Dread Island. In the weird Wild West, Reverend Jebidiah Mercer rides into a possessed town to confront the unspeakable in the crawling sky. Legendary detective C. Auguste Dupin uncovers the gruesome secrets of both the blue lightning bug and the Necronomicon Exploring the darkest corners of the human psyche, here is a lethally entertaining journey through Joe Lansdale’s twisted landscape, where ancient evils lurk and sanity hangs by a rapidly fraying thread. --- J. Todd Kingrea.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L37
НАЗВАНИЕ: J. Todd Kingrea ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:21:53 СИМВОЛОВ: 1055 J. Todd Kingrea Connection to the Mythos Author Artist Games designer Birthplace United States Nationality United States Website J. Todd Kingrea is an American author, pastor, film critic, artist, and games designer. His first published works were articles and story modules for the now-defunct Call of Cthulhu fanzine The Unspeakable Oath , followed by contributions to official Call of Cthulhu works such as Ramsey Campbell's Goatswood and Less Pleasant Places . He has since branched out into the world of short fiction and novels, and his stories have been published in anthologies including Made in Goatswood and Return to Lovecraft Country Selected Works Short Fiction "Cross My Heart, Hope to Die" (1995) Gaming Supplements End of the World (1994, co-author, artist) Dead Reckonings (1998, contributor) Ramsey Campbell's Goatswood and Less Pleasant Places (2001, contributor) The Resurrected III: Out of the Vault (2002, contributor) Artwork Creatures & Cultists (1990, card game) Crypt of Cthulhu (1994, Lammas issue)


--- Jeff Barber.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L59
НАЗВАНИЕ: Jeff Barber ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:22:01 СИМВОЛОВ: 1574 Jeff Barber Full Name Jeffrey "Jeff" Barber Connection to the Mythos Traditional games designer Artist Birthplace Columbia, Missouri, USA Nationality United States Genre Roleplaying games Jeffrey "Jeff" Barber is an American games designer and occasional artist who has worked on several Cthulhu Mythos projects through his involvement with third-party content provider Pagan Publishing . Despite his work on Lovecraftian games, he is best known for his science fiction eco-fantasy Blue Planet roleplaying game. Overview Beginning his career at Pagan Publishing as a member of their volunteer staff , during his time there Barber authored several Cthulhu Mythos works, all of which were published through in-house magazine The Unspeakable Oath , with many articles receiving reprints in later years. When founder John Scott Tynes decided to relocate the company to Seattle, Washington, Barber parted ways with Pagan, moving on to establish his own enterprise, Biohazard Games .

[1] Туманом L37
Спуск оказался долгим. Лестница заворачивалась, обвивая камень, и лишь качающиеся впереди фигуры выделялись в монотонии нескончаемого схождения. Наконец глубоко под землей ступеньки закончились, и через плечи своих проводников я увидел огромную дверь, преградившую наш путь. Она была грубо высечена из цельной породы, а над проемом виднелась загадочная и навевающая странную тревогу резьба из неизвестных мне символов. Дверь распахнулась, я вошел и остановился, всматриваясь в серую мглу. Я стоял на пологом склоне, уходившем на скрытое туманом расстояние, откуда доносились беспорядочно смешанные приглушенные вопли и пронзительные, резкие взвизги, смутно напоминающие бесстыдный смех. Темные мерцающие объекты то выплывали из тумана в поле моего зрения, то исчезали, и большие неясные тени проносились над головой на беззвучных крыльях. Недалеко от меня находился длинный прямоугольный каменный стол, за которым сидело два десятка мужчин, наблюдавших за мной глазами, тускло светящимися в глубоких впадинах. Двое моих проводников безмолвно заняли свои места среди них. Густой туман внезапно начал подниматься. Его небрежно уносило дыханием холодного ветра. Он быстро рассеялся, и стало видно, что вдалеке мгла достигла пещеры.

И все равно выходит какая-то несуразица? Или?... В голове у Кроу по-прежнему стоял туман, или, вернее сказать, в нем образовалась некая бездна, которая отчаянно пыталась чем-то заполнить себя; некая ментальная разрезная картинка, в которой не хватало множества фрагментов, отчего она упорно отказывалась складываться в единое целое. Титус догадывался, что где-то в глубине его сознания наверняка таятся ответы, просто они не желают всплывать на поверхность.

--- Magnum Innominandum.txt ---

[1] The Nameless Mist L69

Lovecraft The Magnum Innominandum (also known as the Nameless Mist Nyog'Sothep or N'yog-Sothep ) is a fictional deity from the Cthulhu Mythos .

[2] Magnum Innominandum L87
P. Lovecraft 4.617 (The Nameless Mist) Appearances HPL The Whisperer in Darkness CIRCLE The Shambler from the Stars Created by H. P. Lovecraft The Magnum Innominandum (also known as the Nameless Mist Nyog'Sothep or N'yog-Sothep ) is a fictional deity from the Cthulhu Mythos . It was first mentioned by H. P.

[3] The Nameless Mist L113
It was first mentioned by H. P. Lovecraft in the Selected Letters Overview The Nameless Mist is an Outer God , who is the progenitor of several other cosmic entities, including Yog-Sothoth .

[5] Magnum Innominandum L137
Behind the Mythos The Magnum Innominandum idea came to Lovecraft during a Roman dream he recalled in three letters, one of those being incorporated into The Horror from the Hills and another posthumously published as " The Very Old Folk ". It was also mentioned in The Whisperer in Darkness , alongside many Mythos concepts such as Hastur , the Yellow Sign and Yog-Sothoth.

[7] Magnum Innominandum L159
It was also mentioned in The Whisperer in Darkness , alongside many Mythos concepts such as Hastur , the Yellow Sign and Yog-Sothoth. The incantation " Tibi, Magnum Innominandum, signa stellarum nigrarum et bufaniformis Sadoquae sigilum " from De Vermis Mysteriis was penned by Lovecraft for Robert Bloch 's " The Shambler from the Stars Lovecraft also mentioned the "Not-to-be-Named One" in " The Mound " as husband of Shub-Niggurath (this has led to the theory that the Magnum Innominandum and the Nameless Mist are two separate entities, and the Magnum Innominandum is Yog-Sothoth). He also introduced "Him Who is Not to Be Named" along with other names such as Shub-Niggurath or Nyarlathothep (though Yog-Sothoth is not named in the invocation).

[8] Him Who is Not to Be Named L181
He also introduced "Him Who is Not to Be Named" along with other names such as Shub-Niggurath or Nyarlathothep (though Yog-Sothoth is not named in the invocation). Lovecraft mentioned the Nameless Mist in a Family Tree of Azathoth . The Nameless Mist was given the name "Nyog'Sothep" and "N'yog-Sothep" by later authors.

[9] The Nameless Mist L187

В вертолете я сидел между (рядовым Майком) Джонсоном и (рядовым Дарреном) Маршем, нашим радистом. Лейтенант Эллери заметил мою нервозность. — Соберись с духом, Грегсон, шанс контакта с противником минимален. Ты еще вернешься назад. — Хотя бы в мешке для трупов, — добавил (капрал Рональд) Лейк, пулеметчик с M60. — Пошел ты, Лейк, — крикнул я в ответ. — Заткнитесь вы оба, — отрезал лейтенант и разговор закончился. Я чувствовал тошноту. Не помню точно, сколько времени мы провели в вертолете, но, похоже, не очень долго, прежде чем он сел на небольшой поляне в раннем утреннем тумане. Я помню, как подумал, что вряд ли можно не услышать рев нашего вертолета при снижении. Это было достаточно громко. Я выпрыгнул последним, и через секунду после того, как мои ноги коснулись земли, вертолет поднялся в воздух и умчался прочь. Эллери выкрикнул короткий приказ принять стандартное построение для разведывательного отряда. Будучи прикрепленным к отряду медиком, я шел в центре рядом с лейтенантом. Сержант (Кристофер) Митчелл был впереди. Несколько часов мы продвигались через плотные вьетнамские джунгли. Каждый шаг, казалось, порождал эхо и объявлял о нашем присутствии по всей территории страны.

НАЗВАНИЕ: Massa di Requiem per Shuggay ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:24:50 СИМВОЛОВ: 1518 Massa di Requiem per Shuggay Author Benvento Chieti Bordighera Country Italy Real World Information First appearance EXP The Unspeakable Oath . . . The opera tells the story of the destruction of Shaggai , and the journeys of its former inhabitants throughout space, a tale filled with death, incest, and rape. Malleus Monstrorum Massa di Requiem per Shuggay Requiem for Shaggai ) is a fictional opera in the Cthulhu Mythos . It first appeared in The Unspeakable Oath , a magazine about Chaosium Call of Cthulhu RPG It is described as a never-published opera composed by Benvento Chieti Bordighera around 1768 in Italy. ( EXP The Encyclopedia Cthulhiana Description The opera describes the flight of the shan from their doomed home world to Earth. Its composition contains lurid and unpleasant scenes depicting torture, incest, and other degradations, and was banned by both the Church and State. ( EXP Malleus Monstrorum ) It also details the shan's worship of Azathoth and a being known as Baoht Z'uqqa-mogg . Performing the entire opera will result in the summoning of Azathoth.

--- Mlandoth.txt ---

[1] Nameless Mist L23
НАЗВАНИЕ: Mlandoth ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:25:29 СИМВОЛОВ: 960 Mlandoth First appearance Where Yidhra Walks Created by Walter C. DeBill Jr. Mlandoth is a primal entity or force, not dissimilar to the Nameless Mist or Darkness , although it is uncertain if it is a place, conscious being, or an inconceivable maelstrom of unknown forces and properties outside the perceptible cosmos. It is mentioned in Uralte Schrecken as a kind of prime archetype from which all mythical god-heads are derived. It is paired with the equally primordial Mril Thorion. According to the cycle surrounding these beings, they are a sort of cosmic yin and yang, whose meeting resulted in the creation of all things (although Azathoth is usually attributed to this). Their joinings routinely create and destroy matter and entities.

--- NONFICTION_6c6202fd50.txt ---

[2] The Unspeakable L87
That river runs under this palace. They have ladders hung over the water where they can hang and fish for the corpses that come floating through . . . At first they seemed like gray stone statues, those motionless shapes, hairy, man-like, yet hideously human; but their eyes were alive, cold sparks of gray icy fire. The fact that Howard mentions the eyes strongly suggests that "The Lurking Fear" may have been of influence, since it is the eyes in Lovecraft's story that give it its final, terrifying clincher: What I saw in the glow of the flashlight after I shot the unspeakable straggling object was so simple that almost a minute elapsed before I understood and went delirious. The object was nauseous; a filthy whitish gorilla thing with sharp yellow fangs and matted fur. It was the ultimate product of mammalian degeneration; the frightful outcome of isolated spawning, multiplication, and cannibal nutrition above and below the ground; the embodiment of all the snarling and chaos and grinning fear that lurk behind life. It had looked at me as it died, and its eyes had the same odd quality that marked those other eyes which had stared at me underground and excited cloudy recollections.

--- Ns12revb_7f8e97ea59.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L27
Like DG, Countdown is a sourcebook, not an anthology of adventures. The bulk of this weighty tome, roughly two hundred pages, is devoted to meticulous background information on eight organizations involved in Mythos activity in some way. Some will clearly be antagonists for Delta Green teams, others may be uneasy allies. In this business, there are no staunch allies. Twenty pages are devoted to the "Hastur Mythos." From the very first issue of The Unspeakable Oath, Pagan Publishing's flagship magazine of Lovecraftian role-playing, it has been clear that John Tynes feels a very personal connection with Hastur and the congeries of ideas that surround Him. This chapter elaborates those earlier articles, and provides a beautiful blueprint for how to send investigators into the domain of the King in Yellow. Then there follow several appendices. The first discusses the introduction of psychic powers into the game. The second consists of several expert scientific reports on Mythos entities prepared by Professor Emerson. These would make excellent prop handouts, when the agents ask their superiors, "What do we know about fish-men?" A short appendix adds a few skills to the repertoire of the DG operative.

--- One Last Task for Athammaus_2 [Перевод [Перевод.txt].txt].txt ---

[4] Туман L83
Одно последнее задание. Старик продолжал путь. Голоса шептали, насмехались. Во тьме двигались фигуры. Кое-кто помимо чёрного фантома, направлявшего его шаги. Многие другие, стоило ему приблизиться, чтобы взглянуть на них. Он видел тела в свете полной луны, что пробивалась сквозь всё более сгущавшийся полог растительности и деревьев. Лунный свет освещал туман, стелившийся по земле, и призрачные очертания тел, безголовых тел. Сперва немногие, затем больше, сотни больше. Тысячи больше. Всё это были безголовые трупы, бесцельно бродившие во тьме, разыскивая свои головы. Атаммаус остановился, смотрел и недоумевал. Слабые отголоски узнавания, смутные и далёкие, в истрёпанных лохмотьях, что были на них. Все они были преступниками.


--- Outer_God_The_H.P._Lovecraft_Wiki_Fandom_d6cc1957a0.txt ---

[4] Magnum Innominandum L425
P. Berglund; Strange Songs, Cabal 13) In Other Media[] In Marvel Comics, the Outer Gods are conflated with the Great Old Ones (also known as the Many-Angled Ones) often enough to be categorized the same. Only two gods have been confirmed as Outer Gods: Yog-Sothoth and Azotharoth. Cthulhu Mythos' Outer Gods mentioned in the Marvel Universe but not confirmed as Outer Gods includes Azathoth (hypothesized to be Azotharoth), Nyarlathotep (an Other God instead), Shub-Niggurath (confirmed to be either a Great Old One or Lesser Old One), Lilith (used in her mythological incarnation instead, though involved in eldritch horror stories), the Magnum Innominandum, and Ubbo-Sathla (unclassified so far). In the Doctor Who universe, the Mythos entities are depicted similarly to how Derleth portrayed them, in that there are no Outer Gods or equivalent, and entities such as Yog-Sothoth and Nyarlathotep figure among the Great Old Ones. Gallery[] Main article: Outer God/Gallery References[] ↑ Wikipedia, "Yaldabaoth."

--- Pagan Publishing.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L59
НАЗВАНИЕ: Pagan Publishing ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:26:50 СИМВОЛОВ: 2178 Pagan Publishing Parent Company Tynes Cowan Corporation Current Status Apparently defunct Founded Founder John Scott Tynes Country United States Headquarters Columbia, Missouri (1990-1994) Seattle, Washington (1994 onwards) Imprints Armitage House Pagan Publishing was a small publishing company whose focus was on Cthulhu Mythos games and related fiction. Overview Founded by John Scott Tynes in 1990 when he was just 19, Pagan Publishing's first product was the Call of Cthulhu fanzine The Unspeakable Oath . As the company grew and expanded it began to produce actual sourcebooks for the game, perhaps the most famous being the Delta Green supplement written by Tynes, Adam Scott Glancy and Dennis Detwiller Since it’s formation, Pagan Publishing has mostly produced content in book form, although it has also dabbled in the field of card games with Creatures & Cultists in 1993 and even released a modular board game in the form of The Hills Rise Wild! in 2000.

--- Professor Peabody's Last Lecture.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L65
The sketch was written by series producer Jack Laird, directed by Jerrold Freedman, and stars Carl Reiner as the titular ill-starred academic. Synopsis A professor of comparative religion gives a lecture on an ancient belief system centered around the Great Old Ones , who supposedly ruled the Earth before mankind. Far from taking the subject seriously, the professor repeatedly comments on how absurd and preposterous the cult of the Old Ones is, and makes several jokes about the deities. When a student points out that mentioning Hastur by name is a taboo, the lecturer dismisses his concerns and speaks the unspeakable's name aloud several more times. At this point, a storm brews in the summer sky outside. The professor grows visibly nervous, but tries to hide it while continuing to make jokes. Finally, he produces a rare copy of the forbidden Necronomicon that he got from Miskatonic University and starts reading it, casually dismissing the book's warning about the "ghastly consequences". The storm rages while the professor continues to read the book aloud, until he is finally transformed into a monstrous creature before the shocked eyes of his students. Behind the Mythos According to Prof.

[3] The Unspeakable L113
Behind the Mythos According to Prof. Peabody, the Great Old Ones include Nyarlathotep 'Umr at-Tawil , Hastur, Cthulhu Azathoth Shub-Niggurath , and Yog-Sothoth . He mentions that their cult has been active as recently as 50 years ago in Arkham , Massachusetts. The three students who ask questions during the lecture are named Mr. Bloch, Mr. Derleth, and Mr. Lovecraft, after seminal Mythos contributors Robert Bloch August Derleth and H. P. Lovecraft . Fittingly, Mr. Derleth is the one who points out that Hastur's name should not be spoken, as it was the real Derleth who came up with the deity's epithet "the Unspeakable". Also fittingly, Mr. Lovecraft is the one who asks questions about the Necronomicon , as the grimoire was Lovecraft's creation in real life. The end credits reveal that another student is named Miss Heald, after Lovecraft's frequent collaborator Hazel Heald Trivia "Professor Peabody's Last Lecture" was the only original Mythos story featured in Night Gallery , but not the only Mythos story overall, as the series also featured adaptations of " Cool Air Pickman's Model " and " The Return of the Sorcerer

--- Shabbith-Ka.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L51
НАЗВАНИЕ: Shabbith-Ka ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:28:22 СИМВОЛОВ: 1704 Shabbith-Ka Species Outer God First appearance EXP The Unspeakable Oath 8/9 Created by Jeff Moeller Shabbith-Ka is a Lesser Outer God which appears in the H. P. Lovecraft -inspired Cthulhu Mythos . Created by Jeff Moeller , it appeared in the Call of Cthulhu roleplaying game scenario "What Goes Around, Comes Around," first published in The Unspeakable Oath 8/9, and later included in the collection The Resurrected III: Out of the Vault Description Shabbith-Ka manifests as an amorphous blob of glowing purple energy; staring at the entity for long enough may allow the viewer to discern the outline of a rudimentary face, although what effect such an action might have on the person's sanity is enough to make one shudder. The entity's worshippers summon it through the use of a particular spell, which is employed in the form of a song. When summoned, anyone not under the protection of an Elder Sign or actively singing the summoning spell will be attacked and consumed by it. It achieves this by absorbing the person into its "body," leaving nothing behind but a purple-tinged husk, which quickly disintegrates into ashes. Due to the nature of its form, physical weapons are useless against Shabbith-Ka.

--- Shambler From the Stars (Cthulhu Universalis).txt ---

[1] Magnum Innominandum L17
НАЗВАНИЕ: Shambler From the Stars (Cthulhu Universalis) ИСТОЧНИК: Shambler From the Stars A familiar or servitor from beyond the stars that can be summoned with an orison or spell from De Vermis Mysteriis . The long and sinister invocation begins with " Tibi, Magnum Innominandum , signa stellarum nigrarum et bufoniformis Sadoquae sigillum.... " Per Google Translate , this apparently means something like "To thee, Great Nameless One, the signs of the black stars and the toad-shaped seal of Sadoqua ( Tsathoggua )". When a mystic dreamer in Providence read the spell aloud, the creature's arrival was heralded by extreme cold and the sound of cunning laughter. The creature was initially invisible when it lifted up the dreamer, tore his neck, and drank his blood.

--- Shane Ivey.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L57
НАЗВАНИЕ: Shane Ivey ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:28:46 СИМВОЛОВ: 1064 Shane Ivey Connection to the Mythos Games designer Editor Nationality United States Genre Roleplaying games Shane Ivey is an American games designer whose output has included numerous contributions to Cthulhu Mythos works. His gaming credits include the core rulebook for the Delta Green stand-alone horror roleplaying game as well as a large number of its supplements, and several books for Cubicle 7 Entertainment 's Tolkenian fantasy The One Ring Roleplaying Game Ivey is also the co-founder, with Dennis Detwiller , of Arc Dream Publishing , through which they publish Delta Green and the resurrected roleplaying magazine The Unspeakable Oath , for which he is the editor-in-chief. He also worked for the now-defunct Pagan Publishing , several of whose works Ivey and Detwiller have continued through Arc Dream. --- Silent Legions.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L43
It was published by Sine Nomine Publishing in 2015. Overview The world is broken in hidden ways. The truth is concealed for the sake of mortal minds. The unendurable reality that boils behind the stars is veiled by the pleasant blindness that human sanity requires. Yet every comforting shroud must fall in time, and for these investigators, that time is now. Silent Legions is a game of modern-day Lovecraftian horror, one carefully designed to support sandbox adventures by hard-bitten investigators and fearless hunters of the dark. Its clean, lightweight old-school rules are compatible with the free Stars Without Number sci-fi game and give both GM and players a steady framework for plumbing the unspeakable secrets of the eldritch night. Now, after a successful Kickstarter with more than 1,100 backers, a Kickstarter completed a month early, this tome of dark secrets can be yours. Silent Legions isn't just about traditional Yog-Sothothery Silent Legions is a creation kit, a toolbox in the Sine Nomine style for creating your own Mythos, with all-new deities, alien entities, foul magics, accursed artifacts, unaussprechlichen kulten, and diabolical dream-worlds to bedevil your players.

--- Stories_2c75e09c87 [Duane W. Rimel and H. P. Lovecraft, The Disinterment.txt].txt ---

[5] The Unspeakable L93
I started erect, horrified. Had some idiot placed my tombstone on the wrong grave, causing me to unearth another body? For surely there could be no mistaking that awful stench. Gradually a hideous uncertainty came over me and I scrambled from the hole. One look at the newly made headpiece was enough. This was indeed my own grave . . . but what fool had buried within it another corpse? All at once a bit of the unspeakable truth propelled itself upon my brain. The odor, in spite of its putrescence, seemed somehow familiar—horribly familiar. . . . Yet I could not credit my senses with such an idea. Reeling and cursing, I fell into the black cavity once more, and by the aid of a hastily lit match, lifted the long lid completely open. Then the light went out, as if extinguished by a malignant hand, and I clawed my way out of that accursed pit, screaming in a frenzy of fear and loathing.

--- Stories_aeb350f487 [H. P. Lovecraft, The Lurking Fear.txt].txt ---

[5] The Unspeakable L171
The thing will haunt me, for who can say the extermination is complete, and that analogous phenomena do not exist all over the world? Who can, with my knowledge, think of the earth’s unknown caverns without a nightmare dread of future possibilities? I cannot see a well or a subway entrance without shuddering... why cannot the doctors give me something to make me sleep, or truly calm my brain when it thunders? What I saw in the glow of my flashlight after I shot the unspeakable straggling object was so simple that almost a minute elapsed before I understood and went delirious. The object was nauseous; a filthy whitish gorilla thing with sharp yellow fangs and matted fur. It was the ultimate product of mammalian degeneration; the frightful outcome of isolated spawning, multiplication, and cannibal nutrition above and below the ground; the embodiment of all the snarling chaos and grinning fear that lurk behind life. It had looked at me as it died, and its eyes had the same odd quality that marked those other eyes which had stared at me underground and excited cloudy recollections.


--- Stories_b3b29928d7 [Robert Bloch, The Shambler from the Stars.txt].txt ---

[4] Magnum Innominandum L513
Here, under a chapter on familiars, he had found an orison or spell, perhaps the very one Prinn had used to call upon his unseen servitors from beyond the stars! Let me listen while he read. I sat there dully, like a stupid, uncomprehending fool. Why did I not scream, try to escape, or tear that monstrous manuscript from his hands? Instead I sat there—sat there while my friend, in a voice cracked with unnatural excitement, read in Latin a long and sonorously sinister invocation. "Tibi, Magnum Innominandum, signa stellarum nigrarum et bufoniformis Sadoquae sigillum...." The croaking ritual proceeded, then rose on wings of nighted, hideous horror. The words seemed to writhe like flames in the air, burning into my brain. The thundering tones cast an echo into infinity, beyond the farthermost star. They seemed to pass into primal and undimensioned gates, to seek out a listener there, and summon him to earth. Was it all an illusion? I did not pause to ponder. For that unwitting summons was answered. Scarcely had my companion's voice died away in that little room before the terror came.

--- The Cackle of Cthulhu.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L67
Edited by Alex Shvartsman , who also contributed one of the stories, it was published by Baen in January 2018. Overview In 1928, Weird Tales debuted " The Call of Cthulhu " by H. P. Lovecraft , and the Cthulhu Mythos was born. In the 90 years since, dozens of writers have dared play within HPL’s mind-blowing creation—but never with such terrifyingly funny results. Now top authors lampoon, parody, and subvert Lovecraft’s Mythos. See Cthulhu cut short his nap at the bottom of the Pacific Ocean to invade North Korea! Watch the Unspeakable Eater of Souls solve crimes on the pulpy streets of Innsmouth ! And speaking of largish Elder Gods , listen to a plastic Elvis doll dispense folksy advice straight from the heart of the Emperor of Dread! Again Ol’ Tentacle-Face is confronted by frail humans who dare defy the Incarnation of Ultimate Evil—but this time not by brave monster hunters and terrified villagers, but by fan fiction writers, clueless college students, and corporate lawyers (okay, we realize it’s hard to know who to root for in that confrontation).

От свирепых поларианских налетчиков и уединенной долины, которую они звали домом, не было обнаружено никаких следов.

--- The Feaster from Afar (story).txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L89
Long ago, Saines says, a branch of the Whateley family lived on these hills, and they summoned something from the sky that never left. When Saines explains that such things are spoken of in the Cthulhu Mythos , Madison indignantly retorts that this whole mythology is fiction made up by an old pulp-magazine writer whose name he can't recall. The next morning, Madison opens a book and finds a note which claims that a cosmic entity named Hastur the Unspeakable has been summoned, and "shall find sustenance". Despite the visible age of the paper, Madison convinces himself that it must be part of a hoax, planted there by Saines or someone else to scare him. At night, Madison experiences one last dream of dashing over the hills, trying to outrun his pursuer. Even though he cannot possibly escape, fear alone keeps him going, until he comes to realise that he isn't actually dreaming, but awake, having sleep-walked away from the lodge into the wilderness.

--- The Importance of Cthulhu (August Derleth's Cthulhu Mythos).txt ---

[3] The Unspeakable L225
There were certain inferior ones, who are in numerical superiority. These are not quite as free as the remaining few, and many of them are subject to many of the same laws which govern mankind. The first among them is Cthulhu , who lies supposedly ‘dead but dreaming’ in the unknown sunken city of R’lyeh , which some writers have thought to be in Atlantis , some in Mu , and some few in the sea not far off the coast of Massachusetts . Second among them is Hastur, sometimes called Him Who Is Not To Be Named and Hastur the Unspeakable, who supposedly resides in Hali in the Hyades . Third is Shub-Niggurath, a horrible travesty on a god or goddess of fertility. Next comes one who is described as the ‘Messenger of the Gods’— Nyarlathotep —and particularly of the most powerful extension of the Great Old Ones, the noxious Yog-Sothoth, who shares the dominion of Azathoth the blind and idiot chaos at the center of infinity. I see by the expression of your eyes that you are beginning to recognize some of these names.

[1] The Unspeakable L79
This fascinating tome traces the secret legacy of The King in Yellow in chronological order, from the bloodline of Edgar Allan Poe to Charles Baudelaire, Gustave Naduad to Ambrose Bierce , Robert Chambers to H. P. Lovecraft , and into the present day with such tributes as TRUE DETECTIVE and A SEASON IN CARCOSA come unravel the mystery of the plague masquerade, the Tatterdemalion, the Pallid Mask Hastur Hali , and the unspeakable riddle of the Yellow Sign This work is one of the volumes of the LOVECRAFTIAN LIBRARIUM, a treasury of the masterworks which H. P. Lovecraft modeled his own writings upon in his development of the Cthulhu Mythos , For readers interested in the very best tales of the supernatural, the Librarium will prove to be one of the finest collections of classic essentials!

Adams Language English Series Translations from the Book of Yng Publication Nightscapes Publication date September 1998 ← Previous Next → The Debt of the Summoner The Fleece of Yaggar The Punishment of Igharta " is a pseudo-factual Cthulhu Mythos work by author and editor F. C. Adams . It forms part of his Translations from the Book of Yng series. Synopsis The story, a fragment of an occult text , tells of how the offspring of Shub-Niggurath , a creature named Igharta, is cursed by Hastur the Unspeakable for the rape of one of his priestesses. He then goes on to sire the line of creatures known as Satyrs Characters Igharta , the child of Shub-Niggurath and a human female. Shub-Niggurath , an Outer God Hastur the Unspeakable , a Great Old One Nyarti , a priestess of Hastur. Latina , a concubine to Shub-Niggurath. "The Punishment of Igharta" was published in the E. P. Berglund -edited online zine Nightscapes in September 1998.

[1] The Unspeakable L75
Written by Jeff Barber and John Tynes , it featured artwork by Barber and Blair Reynolds . The book was published as part of Pagan's sporadically-released series The Resurrected , which reprinted rare and out of print material originally appearing in the gaming magazine The Unspeakable Oath Overview Welcome to the Bottom of the World You are on the sea floor, 1500 feet below the surface. Around you there is only cold and dark — the sun's caressing rays bring nothing to this world. The pressure here is over six hundred pounds per square inch. This is a lethal place. There must be caution in every move you make, or the sea will claim your body and your soul. You and your companions are aboard the RSV Wallaby, a prototype research vessel of your own design. This is its maiden voyage; the attention of the world is upon you. You are not alone.

[1] The Unspeakable L45
НАЗВАНИЕ: The Rise of Hastur ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:32:40 СИМВОЛОВ: 1315 The Rise of Hastur Designer Kira Magrann Lara Turner Publisher Evil Hat Productions Publication Date Genre Roleplaying game System Fate The Rise of Hastur is a timeline supplement for the Fate of Cthulhu supernatural horror roleplaying game. Written by Kira Magrann and Lara Turner , it was published in a digital format by Evil Hat Productions in 2021. Overview Slowly the harbingers of the Unspeakable One rise to infect humanity with despair: An impossibly huge stone tower surrounded by still, black water appears in Utah. Three seemingly-unrelated oil spills shift and transform into massive ritual circles, poisoning animals and humans alike. People across the globe succumb to deathly dreams and sleepwalk into the nearest ocean or lake. Four giant supernatural falcons manifest around the world and spread incomprehensible destruction. In the end, Cassilda the blue flame of the Hyades battles the Unspeakable One in an effort to save the planet from Hastur ’s all-consuming mantle of darkness. But the world is shattered and swallowed.

Now you stand on the brink of the Apocalypse, with one goal: Beat back the oily waters of the Unspeakable One and save the world from the alien pull of the Black Star. Publishers description

--- The Torturers of Zykakis [Перевод.txt].txt ---

[1] Туманах L9
Зиилан трудился под началом архимага Зикакиса, и его обязанности сводились исключительно к работе с теми, кто вызвал недовольство его господина. Не то чтобы его господин был неспособен выполнять эти обязанности сам, его искусства были таковы, что любую пытку можно было провести с лёгкостью, и они простирались до крайностей боли, которых даже великое мастерство Зиилана не могло достичь, однако Зикакис сдерживал эти силы, храня их для более великой цели. Зиилан долгое время был мучителем высокого положения, его опыт простирался ещё до его рождения, ибо это было семейное ремесло, передаваемое от отца к сыну в забытых туманах его родословной. Это было искусство, которое он сам передавал своим сыновьям. Его пытки были худшего рода, начинаясь с самых нежных ласк и переходя к чрезмерностям боли, способным удерживать человека в сознательной агонии днями, не предлагая спасения ни в туманах беспамятства, ни даже в самой смерти. Хуже того были ментальные пытки, в которых он преуспевал, — пытки, ведущие жертву по пути предательства всего, что ей было дорого.

[2] Туманах L11
Зиилан долгое время был мучителем высокого положения, его опыт простирался ещё до его рождения, ибо это было семейное ремесло, передаваемое от отца к сыну в забытых туманах его родословной. Его пытки были худшего рода, начинаясь с самых нежных ласк и переходя к чрезмерностям боли, способным удерживать человека в сознательной агонии днями, не предлагая спасения ни в туманах беспамятства, ни даже в самой смерти. Он извлекал секреты, которые не были секретами, мимолётные приятные мысли, моменты душевной близости, тайны, являющиеся тайнами лишь потому, что их никогда не выражают осознанно, а лишь хранят. Всё это он предлагал Зикакису, для которого каждое мгновение чужой боли было изысканным наслаждением. Он часто сидел и наблюдал за работой своего любимого художника.

--- The Unspeakable Betrothal.txt ---

[1] The Unspeakable L1
НАЗВАНИЕ: The Unspeakable Betrothal ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:33:31 СИМВОЛОВ: 1063 The Unspeakable Betrothal Author Robert Bloch Language English Publisher Avon Publication date "The Unspeakable Betrothal" is a short story by Robert Bloch that originally appeared in Avon Fantasy Reader No. 9 (1949), edited by Donald A. Wollheim. It tells the story of Avis Long, a girl, and later a woman, obsessed with creatures from her dreams and the round window in her bedroom. The creatures from Avis's dreams come from Yuggoth , which is described as "not beyond the rim of stars but between it". Characters Avis Long: An orphan girl who spends much of her childhood in her bedroom, immersed in fantasy and later in dreams that "poured down from the sky, through the window".
